Mixed language, cache and history qualification

Preserved failed catalog/Fortify qualification.
This exact 54-process implementation passes PHP pressure, original-source pressure and 24-commit history, but fails Large mixed cold/warm/history time and Large legacy cold/warm time guards. Complete graphs and stored snapshots agree. Unchanged 10% guards and all original measurements remain retained. Host activity was observed; no cause is established or guard waived. Runtime qualification remains open.

P10d2b2b2b1 · 2026-10-12T05:35:24.845Z · v22.12.0 · linux/x64 · AMD Ryzen 7 5800H with Radeon Graphics. 3 alternating fresh processes per implementation and size; compiled-cli. The monorepo combines all eighteen Tree-sitter structural adapters, PHP, TypeScript and original Vue/Svelte/Astro sources; Express/Nest, FastAPI/Flask/Django, Rails/Laravel, eight Go router profiles, Java/Kotlin MVC/WebFlux and ASP.NET 8/9/10, and six Rust router/version profiles. Recorded target inputs qualify source contracts on Linux; they do not establish native Windows/macOS execution or framework runtime validity. Target dependencies, toolchains, applications, plugins and hooks never run. The literal fixtures are authored qualification inputs, not upstream repository accuracy evidence.

Cold scans have no cache. Population, warm and a shared Node dependency edit are timed separately. Both original consumer call targets bind to the shared source; the edit invalidates their connected cache unit while unrelated TypeScript units stay hits. Warm graph, syntax and semantic cache events are recorded by analyzer; a qualified PHP graph hit skips native-tree hydration. The cache keeps at most two versions per unit, 256 MiB compressed overall and 64 MiB decoded per entry; oversized/corrupt entries fall back to source rather than blocking indexing.

The 4-commit history edits the shared dependency and an original PHP body, then renames a Vue component; deeper revisions also edit and repeatedly rename an original Python file. Two history workers materialize original Git trees; snapshots include complete entities, relations and diagnostics, not just counts. Only working-tree Git metrics (including absence after removing all Git-only fields) and Git-availability diagnostics are normalized for live-versus-revision comparisons. All semantic metadata, evidence and original ranges remain in the digest.

A separate process polls every thread's descendant list every 25 ms, with an acknowledged start marker before synchronous work. RSS sums include history and parser children; shared pages can be counted repeatedly, exiting processes can make a poll incomplete, and peaks between polls can be missed. Raw parent VmHWM is cumulative since exec, not an isolated warm/history peak. Source memos are limited to 32 MiB of conservative UTF-16 text/key payload and 4,096 entries per instance; compiler/semantic consumers can retain their own text and ASTs, so this is not an RSS cap. Each historical worker now waits until the parent stores or records failure for its one graph before analyzing another commit. The ordinary sample remains below the byte limit; a separate source-pressure profile exercises eviction. Indexed native jobs reserve at most six primary/auxiliary grammars or 16 MiB cumulative original UTF-8 source per generation, then await its exit before a successor. One allowed larger original remains analyzable. Dispatch groups template-host and C-family grammars, then remaining grammars and original paths, so different applications reuse loaded grammars without changing per-file identities. Ordinary originals and their memos are released immediately after declaration consumption; compact deferred template jobs also release their original memo before the next native job. Deferred scripts reread and validate original bytes after the native phase, then release that memo after consumption. Indexing awaits PHP parser retirement too, so no parser child survives completion into warm replay or the next historical revision. Once all declarations and deferred scripts are consumed and parser retirement completes, the shared source memo is cleared; subsequent consumers validate any needed rereads through the indexed boundary. Main-process per-original release counts, phase retirement counts and released payloads are recorded below. The superseded retained-source characterization preserves all 60 completed observations, including its failed warm-pressure memory guard. Phase-end clearing alone also failed; its full sixty-process record and preceding narrowly passing characterization remain available. Native observations cover main-process indexing only; history children follow the same policy. These thresholds bound lifetimes, not native allocation or RSS. Per-commit RSS checkpoints are observations after result delivery, not instantaneous or independent peaks. Native OS readers, broader AST/consumer budgets, much longer production histories and production-repository accuracy remain additional P10d gates. PHP provider-owned residency accounts twice the original native serialized AST payload plus UTF-16 source/key text, capped at 32 MiB or 4,096 units. Evicted units use checksum-verified private ephemeral snapshots, limited to 256 MiB or 4,096 entries; unavailable snapshots reparse validated originals. Six source reads/native jobs share a 16 MiB original-input reservation, with one allowed larger original admitted alone. PHP binding prepares original site ranges and standalone UTF-16 expressions capped at 2,048 code units, then retires each full original before reading the next. Detached strings preserve a truncated surrogate and cannot hold a full original through a V8 sliced string. Its numeric original/expression ownership observations cover main-process emission; historical children apply the same policy. The retained-source predecessor preserves all 54 observations, including its +11.15% PHP-pressure history-memory failure. That failed comparison remains available. PHP binding now keeps a detached declaration/signature catalog and compiles/emits scopes for one validated original at a time. Body, statement, comment and parser-owned references cannot escape into that catalog. Laravel retains indexed identities and detached class/method headers; migrations, Fortify, console jobs and effects hydrate validated originals as needed. Route includes compile into detached ordered operations before descending, preserving depth-first registration order without retaining parent originals. Its identity registry owns zero trees and zero original bytes. Repository-wide semantic/graph bytes and other embedded compiler/WASM consumers remain separate budgets; these ownership limits do not cap decoded-object overhead or physical RSS. Main-process PHP counters are recorded; historical children use the same policy. This report records its named phase against the archived baseline.
